# 🔍 Fleet 1108 Audit: Norm Artifacts vs Target Specs (lane `audit-norm`)

Read-only audit, 2026-11-08, ticket `26/11/01/NORM-COMPLETE-VISUAL-EUROCODE-PLUGINS`. Generated by `🧰️scripts/fleet-1108-audit-norm.py` and `🧰️scripts/fleet-1108-audit-norm-report.py`. No tests or builds were run.

## Method

- Spec items: the `items` of each artifact's `🧭️spec-trace/🔣️.json` (795 items in 10 artifacts). The check list per item (`checks` prefixes, design-case `entry`, data `path`) is the claimed realisation. The spec-trace is the repo's self-declaration; this audit verifies it against the source.
- Implemented: the check prefix or entry id occurs as a token (or, for field-path references such as `.profile.siteResponse`, as a substring) in a non-test, non-oracle, non-spec-trace file of that artifact. Hit = `file:line` of the first occurrence. Data items: declared path checked for existence under the subset root.
- Language-agnostic test: the id occurs in a `.feature`, `.json`, `.toml` or `.csv` under a test/fixture path. A `specific` hit = only in a `.rs`/`.ts`/`.py` test file. Only the first 2 hits per item are stored by the script, so a `specific` label can hide a later agnostic hit.
- Third-party oracle: the id occurs in a file under an `oracle` path. Python oracles are classified by their imports (stdlib-only / third-party names). Other languages: unknown.
- Limits: string presence, not behaviour. `no hit` means the id string does not occur in that category. Fixture or catalogue JSON containing an id is counted as a test hit even if no test executes it. Clause-number coverage is not checked.
- Spec-trace vs target spec: see the section `Spec-trace ids versus target markdown`.

## Spec-trace ids versus target markdown

- Every spec-trace id of all 10 artifacts occurs as a boundary-delimited string in its target spec file (checked by substring match, 795 of 795).
- Bold-heading check for EN 1990/1991 (`**ID` at line start): trace ids without a bold heading are E16.04, E16.05, E16.06, E17.10, E3.06, E4.05, E4.06, E4.07, E4.08. They occur in the target text, but not as headings. Each needs a manual look at the target clause.
- A heading-level scan of the other target specs found no trace id missing from the headings, except false positives from table rows and hand-check notes. The reverse direction (heading ids missing from the trace) was not verified exhaustively for EN 1997/1998 (`GI-17` is the only heading-level candidate) or for EN 1993 (`V-029`, `V-255`-`V-260`, `V-301`-`V-305`, `V-424`-`V-427`, `V-806` occur inline, not as headings).
